\displaystyle=\langle\rho_{0},K_{t}^{\ast}\ast\phi(t)-\phi(0)\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\rho(s),V_{1}(s)D_{y}K_{t-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(t)\rangle\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}D_{y}K(t-s,x-y)\,V_{2}(s,dy)\phi(t,x)\,dx\,ds

\displaystyle=\langle\rho_{0},K_{t}^{\ast}\ast\phi(t)-\phi(0)\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\rho(s)V_{1}(s)+V_{2}(s),D_{y}K_{t-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(t)\rangle\,ds=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

Then,

(C.7)

\displaystyle I_{1}=\lim\limits_{s\to 0^{+}}\langle\rho_{0},K_{t}^{\ast}\ast\phi(t)-K_{s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(s)\rangle=\lim\limits_{s\to 0^{+}}\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{t}\langle\rho_{0},K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)\rangle\,d\tau.

We will now justify the above equalities. In the first of them we have used that
\phi\in C([0,T],C^{n}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and that
\phi(0)
and its spatial derivatives of order up to
n
are bounded and
uniformly continuous in
x
, so that
K_{s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(s)\to\phi(0)
in
C^{n}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
To see that the second equality in (
C.7
) is valid, we will show that the time derivative appearing there exists and is bounded and continuous. For
\tau\in(s,t)
and
h\neq 0
small we have

(C.8)

\displaystyle\frac{K_{\tau+h}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau+h)-K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)}{h}=\frac{K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast(\phi(\tau+h)-\phi(\tau))}{h}+\frac{(K_{\tau+h}^{\ast}-K_{\tau}^{\ast})\ast\phi(\tau+h)}{h}.

By
(K)
and the fact that
\partial_{t}\phi\in C_{b}((0,T),C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

the first term in (
C.8
) converges to
K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\partial_{t}\phi(\tau)
in
C^{n}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
h\to 0
.
Indeed, we have
|(\phi(\tau+h,x)-\phi(\tau,x))/h-\partial_{t}\phi(\tau,x)|=|\partial_{t}\phi(\tau_{x},x)-\partial_{t}\phi(\tau,x)|\leq\omega(h)
, so
\|(\phi(\tau+h)-\phi(\tau))/h-\partial_{t}\phi(\tau)\|_{\infty}\to 0
as
h\to 0
. Therefore

(C.9)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast(\phi(\tau+h)-\phi(\tau))}{h}-K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\partial_{t}\phi(\tau)\bigg\|_{C^{n}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\Big\|\frac{\phi(\tau+h)-\phi(\tau)}{h}-\partial_{t}\phi(\tau)\Big\|_{\infty}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\|D^{k}K^{\ast}_{\tau}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits_{h\to 0}0.

By
(K)
, the fact that
(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}^{\ast})K_{t}^{\ast}=0
, and the fact that

\phi\in C([0,T],C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

we find that the second term of (
C.8
) converges to
\partial_{t}K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)
in
C^{n}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
h\to 0^{+}
.
In order to see that we split:

(C.10)

\displaystyle\frac{(K_{\tau+h}^{\ast}-K_{\tau}^{\ast})\ast\phi(\tau+h)}{h}=\frac{(K_{\tau+h}^{\ast}-K_{\tau}^{\ast})}{h}\ast(\phi(\tau+h)-\phi(\tau))+\frac{(K_{\tau+h}^{\ast}-K_{\tau}^{\ast})\ast\phi(\tau)}{h}.

and since

\frac{(K_{\tau+h}^{\ast}-K_{\tau}^{\ast})}{h}-\partial_{\tau}K^{\ast}_{\tau}=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\tau}(K^{*}_{\tau+\lambda h}-K^{*}_{\tau})\,d\lambda=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\tau}^{2}K^{\ast}_{\tau+\lambda\mu h}\,\lambda h\,d\mu\,d\lambda
,
it follows that for
0\leq k\leq n
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|D^{k}\frac{(K_{\tau+h}^{\ast}-K_{\tau}^{\ast})}{h}-D^{k}\partial_{\tau}K^{\ast}_{\tau}\bigg\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq h\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\|D^{k}\mathcal{L}^{2}K^{\ast}_{\tau+\lambda\mu h}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,d\mu\,d\lambda,

which goes to 0 as
h\to 0
. Thus we see that the first term on the right-hand side of (
C.10
) vanishes and the second term converges to
\partial_{t}K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)
by an estimate similar to (
C.9
).
This proves that
\partial_{t}\langle\rho_{0},K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)\rangle=\langle\rho_{0},\partial_{t}(K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau))\rangle
exists and is continuous and bounded, therefore (
C.7
) is valid.

Using
(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}^{\ast})K_{\tau}^{\ast}=0
,
K_{\tau}^{\ast}\in C_{0}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
K_{\tau}^{\ast},\mathcal{L}^{\ast}K_{\tau}^{\ast}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and

\phi(\tau)\in C^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we get
\partial_{t}K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)=K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast\mathcal{L}\phi(\tau)
.
Therefore, for every
\tau\in(0,T]

we find that

\displaystyle I_{1}=\lim\limits_{s\to 0^{+}}\int_{s}^{t}\langle\rho_{0},K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast(\partial_{t}\phi(\tau)+\mathcal{L}\phi(\tau))\rangle\,d\tau.

Furthermore, since

\partial_{t}\phi+\mathcal{L}\phi\in B([0,T],C^{n-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
,
by
(K)
we get

\displaystyle\|K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L})\phi(\tau)\|_{C^{n}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C\tau^{-\frac{1}{\underline{\alpha}}}\|(\partial_{t}+\mathcal{L})\phi(\tau)\|_{C^{n-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\quad\tau\in(0,T).

Therefore, by the dominated convergence theorem,

\displaystyle I_{1}=\int_{0}^{t}\langle\rho_{0},K_{\tau}^{\ast}\ast(\partial_{t}\phi(\tau)+\mathcal{L}\phi(\tau))\rangle\,d\tau.

In
I_{2}
we add and subtract a term with
D_{y}\phi(s)
:

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\langle\rho(s)V_{1}(s)+V_{2}(s),D_{y}K_{t-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(t)-D_{y}\phi(s)\rangle\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\rho(s)V_{1}(s)+V_{2}(s),D_{y}\phi(s)\rangle\,ds=:J_{1}+J_{2}.

We have

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\lim\limits_{h\to 0^{+}}\langle\rho(s)V_{1}(s)+V_{2}(s),D_{y}K_{t-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(t)-D_{y}K_{h}^{\ast}\ast\phi(s+h)\rangle\,ds

(C.11)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\lim\limits_{h\to 0^{+}}\int_{s+h}^{t}\partial_{\tau}\langle\rho(s)V_{1}(s)+V_{2}(s),D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)\rangle\,d\tau\,ds.

The first equality is true because for every
s\in(0,T]
we have
V_{2}(s)\in C^{-0}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\rho(s)V_{1}(s)\in C^{-n+1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(by the mild solution estimates of Lemma
5.7
). Therefore, since
\phi\in C([0,T],C^{n}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
with
D^{n}\phi
uniformly continuous, we get that
K_{h}^{\ast}\ast D_{y}\phi(s+h)\to D_{y}\phi(s)
in
C^{n-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The second equality is justified by showing that the derivative exists and is continuous and bounded, similar to what was done in
I_{1}
: for
\tau\in(s,t)
and
h
small we have

\displaystyle\frac{D_{y}K_{\tau+h-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau+h)-D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)}{h}=\frac{(D_{y}K_{\tau+h-s}^{\ast}-D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast})\ast\phi(\tau+h)}{h}+\frac{D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast(\phi(\tau+h)-\phi(\tau))}{h}.

For the same reasons as before this expression converges in
C^{n-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
to

\partial_{\tau}D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)+D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast\partial_{\tau}\phi(\tau)\in C([s,t],C^{n-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, therefore the
\tau
-derivative in (
C.11
) exists and is continuous and bounded. Furthermore, by the dominated convergence theorem,
(K)
,
(\partial_{t}-\mathcal{L}^{\ast})K_{\tau}^{\ast}=0
,
and
\partial_{t}\phi+\mathcal{L}\phi\in B([0,T],C^{n-1}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
,
we get that

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\langle\rho(s)V_{1}(s)+V_{2}(s),\partial_{\tau}D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast\phi(\tau)+D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast\partial_{\tau}\phi(\tau)\rangle\,d\tau\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\langle\rho(s)V_{1}(s)+V_{2}(s),D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast(\mathcal{L}\phi(\tau)+\partial_{\tau}\phi(\tau))\rangle\,d\tau\,ds

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\tau}\langle\rho(s)V_{1}(s)+V_{2}(s),D_{y}K_{\tau-s}^{\ast}\ast(\mathcal{L}\phi(\tau)+\partial_{\tau}\phi(\tau))\rangle\,ds\,d\tau.

We finish the proof by adding
I_{1},J_{1}
, and
J_{2}
and by again using the definition of mild solution.
∎

Appendix D
Stability of the MFG system with respect to the initial time

Let
h>0
and let
(u_{h},m_{h})
be the solution to the MFG system with initial measure
m_{0}
on the time interval
(t_{0}-h,T)
. We also denote by
(u,m)
the solution on
(t_{0},T)
. We will use the following lemma on stability of the MFG system under the changes of the initial time.

Lemma D.1
.

Let
\sigma\in(0,\underline{\alpha})
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\sigma)
. There exist a constant
C>0
and a modulus of continuity
\omega
independent of
m_{0}
, such that for every
t_{0}\in[0,T)
and
h\in(0,1)
, there is a constant
C>0
independent of
h
such that

(D.1)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\|u_{h}(t,\cdot)-u(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{3+\sigma}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup\limits_{t\in[t_{0},T]}d_{0}(m_{h}(t),m(t))\leq Ch^{\frac{1}{2}},

(D.2)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\in[t_{0}-h,t_{0}]}\|u_{h}(t,\cdot)-u(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{C^{3+\sigma-\varepsilon}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup\limits_{t\in[t_{0}-h,t_{0}]}d_{0}(m_{h}(t),m_{0})\leq\omega(h).

Proof.

Note that
(u,m)
and
(u_{h},m_{h})
solve the MFG system on
(t_{0},T)
with the initial measures
m_{0}
and
m_{h}(t_{0})
respectively. Therefore, by using Theorem
5.3
we get that

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\|u_{h}(t,\cdot)-u(t,\cdot)\|_{C^{3+\sigma}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sup\limits_{t\in[t_{0},T]}d_{0}(m_{h}(t),m(t))\leq Cd_{0}(m_{0},m_{h}(t_{0})).

Furthermore, by Theorem
3.9
(b) we get that

\displaystyle d_{0}(m_{0},m_{h}(t_{0}))\leq c_{0}(1+\|D_{p}H(\cdot,u_{h},Du_{h})\|_{\infty})h^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
u_{h}
and
Du_{h}
are bounded independently of
h
(see Theorem
4.3
), we obtain (
D.1
).

In order to get (
D.2
) we first use the triangle inequality. For every
t\in[t_{0}-h,t_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\|u_{h}(t,\cdot)-u(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{C^{3+\sigma-\varepsilon}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+d_{0}(m_{h}(t),m_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{h}(t,\cdot)-u_{h}(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{C^{3+\sigma-\varepsilon}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+d_{0}(m_{h}(t),m_{h}(t_{0}))

\displaystyle+\|u_{h}(t_{0},\cdot)-u(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{C^{3+\sigma-\varepsilon}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+d_{0}(m_{h}(t_{0}),m_{0}).

The estimate for the last two terms follows from (
D.1
). By Theorem
3.9
(b) we get

\displaystyle d_{0}(m_{h}(t),m_{h}(t_{0}))\leq ch^{\frac{1}{2}},

and by the last part of Theorem
3.10
,

\displaystyle\|u_{h}(t,\cdot)-u_{h}(t_{0},\cdot)\|_{C^{3+\sigma-\varepsilon}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\omega(t_{0}-t)\leq\omega(h),

which ends the proof of (
D.2
).
∎
